We all had to wear these thick wool sweaters under our smocks. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I can use clippers all day long. Buzz cuts, fades, whatever, no problem. But when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station, I just... I get this overwhelming feeling that I’m about to hurt somebody. Or like I'm going to slip and cut myself really badly. It's just sitting there, and I can't stop thinking about the blade.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of harming someone or making a mistake limited to when you're using the straight razor, or do you worry about it with other tools or situations at work as well?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the straight razor. I mean, I use scissors all day, clippers, trimmers... none of that bothers me. I can do a skin fade in my sleep. Actually, I had this guy come in last week who wanted this super intricate design shaved into the side of his head—like a geometric pattern thing. It took me almost an hour just with the trimmers. He was a graphic designer or something, worked for this start-up downtown that makes an app for... I don't even know, dog walking? He showed me the app, it had this little cartoon corgi as the logo. My sister used to have a corgi, named him Waffles. Cutest dog ever, but he shed everywhere. You'd find dog hair in your food, in your shoes... she ended up having to buy this crazy expensive vacuum cleaner just to keep up with it. One of those ones that looks like a spaceship, you know? \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't worry about hurting anyone with the scissors or the clippers. It's literally just the straight razor. When it's just laying there open, that's when my brain goes to that dark place.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of using the straight razor affecting other parts of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't really encounter straight razors anywhere else, you know? Unless I'm at an antique store or something, but I don't really go to those. My girlfriend loves them, though. We went to this huge antique mall last weekend, the kind that’s in an old converted barn? It smelled like dust and old books. She spent like two hours looking at these vintage Pyrex bowls. The ones with the little flowers on the side, you know? Apparently, they're super collectible now. Some of them go for hundreds of dollars online, which is crazy to me because my grandma used to have the exact same ones and she just kept leftover meatloaf in them. My grandma made the best meatloaf, too. She put a little bit of brown sugar in the ketchup glaze on top. It sounds weird, but it caramelized in the oven and it was incredible. She used to serve it with these mashed potatoes that had way too much butter in them, but nobody complained. \n\nBut, uh, no, the razor thing doesn't really bother me at home or anything. Just at the shop when I have to use it.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social situations or activities outside of work because of your fear of straight razors?\n\nPatient: No, no, I still go out and do stuff. Like I said, I don't really run into straight razors at a bar or a movie theater, right? So it doesn't stop me from seeing my friends or my girlfriend. Actually, me and my buddies are supposed to go to this axe-throwing place this weekend. Have you ever done that? It's wild. You stand in these little cages and just chuck a hatchet at a wooden target. First time I went, I was terrible at it. The axe kept bouncing off the wood and flying back at me. The instructor said it was all in the wrist, like skipping a rock. I used to be so good at skipping rocks when I was a kid. We lived near this lake, Lake Winnebago, and my dad would take me out there on Sunday mornings. The water was always so still, like glass. He taught me how to find the perfect flat stone. You want one that's smooth, but not too heavy. My dad had this crazy record, he could get a rock to skip like twelve times. I think my best was maybe seven. He always wore this ridiculous bucket hat when we went to the lake, covered in fishing lures. He thought it was hilarious, but my mom hated it. She actually tried to throw it away once, but he fished it out of the trash... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, social stuff is fine. I'm not avoiding going out or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of the straight razor related to the fact that it's used for shaving, or would you be just as afraid of it if you were using it for something else?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure, honestly. I've never really thought about using it for anything else. I mean, it's a straight razor, so shaving is pretty much all it's good for, right? Well, I guess you could cut stuff with it, like opening boxes or something. Speaking of boxes, we got this massive shipment of new pomades at the shop yesterday. The boxes were huge, and they were packed with all these little styrofoam peanuts. I hate those things. They stick to everything because of the static electricity. You try to throw them away and they just cling to your hand, your shirt, the trash can... it's a nightmare. We had to spend twenty minutes just sweeping up all the packing peanuts before we could even unpack the product. The pomade is pretty nice, though. It's got this sort of matte finish, smells like sandalwood and... maybe a little bit of bergamot? My old roommate used to burn this sandalwood incense all the time. He was super into meditation and yoga and all that. He had this whole shrine set up in the corner of the living room with crystals and singing bowls. He tried to teach me how to meditate once, but I just couldn't clear my mind, you know? I kept thinking about what I was gonna have for dinner. I think we ordered Thai food that night. Pad Thai, extra spicy... \n\nBut, uh, to answer your question, I don't know if it's just the shaving part. I think it's just the razor itself. Just the way it looks when it's open.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel afraid of the straight razor if you were using it for a task that didn't involve shaving?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think so. Even if I was just using it to open one of those boxes I was talking about, it wouldn't matter. It's just... I know it’s just a tool, you know? I went to school for this, I trained with it, and I can use clippers and shears all day long without batting an eye. But when that straight razor is just sitting there, open at my station... man, it just feels like I’m about to hurt somebody, or like I'm gonna slip and cut myself really badly. It's crazy because I've never even nicked anyone before. Well, except this one time in barber college, but that was with scissors, not a razor. We were practicing on these mannequin heads first, right? They had real human hair, which was always a little creepy to me. I always wondered where they got the hair from. Like, do people donate it specifically for mannequins, or is it just leftover from wig makers? Anyway, my buddy in class, Steve, he accidentally cut off a chunk of his own thumb while texturizing the mannequin's hair. Bled everywhere. The instructor had to wrap it up in this huge bandage, and Steve couldn't cut hair for a week. He ended up dropping out a month later to go work at his uncle's auto body shop. I saw him a couple of years ago at a gas station, he looked good. He was driving this vintage Mustang that he restored himself. Cherry red, white leather interior... beautiful car. I've always wanted a classic car, maybe a '69 Charger. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, it doesn't matter what I'm using the razor for. Just seeing it open like that, it just... it terrifies me that someone's gonna get hurt.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be afraid of the straight razor if it were blunt and couldn't cut anyone?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? If it was completely blunt and couldn't cut anything... I guess it wouldn't be as bad, but it would still look the same, you know? Just that shape of it. It's funny you mention blunt things, because my mom used to have these butter knives that were so old and worn down they couldn't even cut hot butter. We had this big Sunday breakfast tradition at my house growing up. Pancakes, bacon, the whole nine yards. My dad made the best pancakes. He'd put chocolate chips in them, but only on my brother's side of the griddle, because I hated chocolate chips in my pancakes. I liked blueberries. Fresh ones, not the frozen kind that turn the whole pancake purple. We used to go berry picking every summer at this farm upstate. You'd get these little green baskets and just fill them up. My brother would eat half of what he picked before we even got to the weigh station. The farmer always knew, but he never said anything. He was this old guy with a huge white beard, looked exactly like Santa Claus in overalls. He had a tractor that he let us sit on once. It was a John Deere, bright green. I always thought tractors were so cool when I was a kid. I wanted to be a farmer for like a year, until I realized how early they have to wake up. I am definitely not a morning person. Even now, getting to the barbershop by 8 AM is a struggle. I usually need at least two cups of coffee before I can even hold a conversation. \n\nBut, um, what were we talking about? Oh, the blunt razor. Yeah, I don't know. I think just seeing it open would still give me that panic, even if I knew it was dull.\n\nDoctor: Would your fear of the straight razor change if you were using it for a task that is very similar to shaving but not actually shaving, such as shaving cream off a balloon like you did in training?\n\nPatient: Shaving a balloon... man, I haven't thought about that since barber college. We used to go through so many balloons. Just those cheap ones from the dollar store. My instructor, Mr. Davis, he'd blow them up so tight that if you even breathed wrong with the blade, they'd pop and shaving cream would go everywhere. I actually hate the smell of that cheap foam shaving cream now. It reminds me of my grandpa, though. He used to use it every morning. He had this little badger hair brush and a ceramic mug. He was a mechanic, so his hands were always covered in grease, but his face was always perfectly smooth. He worked on airplanes, actually. Commercial jets. We got to go to the hangar once when I was like ten. It's crazy how massive those planes are up close. You don't realize it when you're sitting inside them, eating those tiny bags of pretzels. I always ask for the Biscoff cookies instead, those things are amazing with coffee. I tried to buy a box of them at the grocery store once, but they just didn't taste the same on the ground. Maybe it's the altitude? I read somewhere that your taste buds get numb when you're flying. \n\nBut, um... wait, the balloon thing. Honestly, I'm not really sure if it would change anything. I don't think it would. Because I'd still be holding the razor, and it would still be open in my hand. So yeah, I don't know if what I'm shaving actually makes a difference to the panic.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor would decrease if you were using it in a situation where there was no possibility of harming anyone?\n\nPatient: I don't know, honestly. Like if I was alone in a room with it? I guess... I mean, I'd still be holding it, right? And I'd still be the one holding it, so I could still slip and cut myself. My hands get so sweaty when I even think about it. I have to use that talcum powder we have at the shop just to dry them off sometimes. We use this specific brand, Clubman Pinaud. It has that classic barbershop smell, you know? Sort of powdery and masculine. My dad used to wear a cologne that smelled just like it. Old Spice, I think. Or maybe Brut? He always got those gift sets for Christmas with the aftershave and the deodorant. I still buy him one every year, just out of habit. He's retired now, lives down in Florida. Plays golf every single day. I don't get the appeal of golf, honestly. It just seems like a lot of walking in the hot sun to hit a tiny ball. I'd rather be fishing. We used to go fishing on this lake when I was a teenager. Caught a massive largemouth bass once, must have been five pounds. \n\nBut, uh, what were you asking? Oh, if nobody could get hurt. I don't know, I just don't think there's any situation where nobody could get hurt if I'm holding an open razor. I'm there, so I could get hurt. It's just the razor itself, I think.\n\nDoctor: Would your fear of the straight razor be less intense if you were to use it for shaving a mannequin or a dummy, rather than a real person?\n\nPatient: A mannequin? Man, I haven't seen one of those since barber college either. We had these heads we called \"pivot points,\" they had this weirdly realistic hair that you could actually cut and style. Some guys would get so attached to their mannequins, they'd name them and talk to them while they were cutting. There was this one guy, Tommy, he named his \"Brenda\" and he'd actually get mad if someone else touched her. Tommy was a trip. He ended up dropping out and moving to Vegas to become a dealer at one of the casinos. Blackjack, I think. I went to Vegas once for my 21st birthday. We stayed at the Luxor, you know, the giant pyramid? It was so confusing inside, the elevators go up at an angle. We got so lost trying to find our room the first night. We ended up at this buffet at like 3 AM eating prime rib and crab legs. The crab legs were okay, but I've had way better in Maryland. My aunt lives in Annapolis, and she makes this crab dip that is just unbelievable. She puts Old Bay seasoning in everything. She even puts it on popcorn, which sounds gross, but it's actually really good if you have a cold beer with it. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the dummy. I don't know, honestly. Probably not. Because, like I was saying, it's just the razor itself. It doesn't really matter what's in front of me, real person or plastic head. If I'm holding that blade open, I'm just terrified I'm going to slip and slice my own hand open or something. So no, I don't think it would be any less intense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor would decrease if someone else was holding it, but you were still in the room?\n\nPatient: If someone else was holding it... yeah, I think so. If I'm not the one holding it, it's not as bad. Like, the other guys at the shop use them all the time. My buddy Marcus, he's in the chair next to mine, he does these incredibly sharp line-ups with his. He's got this custom-made handle, it's like a dark walnut wood with his initials engraved on it. He paid a fortune for it from this guy on Instagram who makes them by hand. Instagram is crazy for stuff like that now. You can find people making anything. I follow this one account where a guy just restores old axes. He finds them rusted in barns and polishes them up until they look brand new. It's weirdly relaxing to watch. He uses this special grinding wheel, and the sparks fly everywhere. Reminds me of my high school shop class. We had to make a birdhouse, which was so cliché, but I actually tried to make mine look like a miniature log cabin. I spent weeks cutting little dowels for the logs. A squirrel ended up moving into it instead of a bird, though. It chewed the hole bigger and just took over. Squirrels are relentless, man. They get into the bird feeders at my parents' house, and my dad has tried every trick in the book to keep them out. He even put hot sauce on the seeds once, but I think they actually liked it. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, sorry. If Marcus is holding his razor, or anyone else is, I'm fine. I can stand right next to him and talk to him while he's using it. It's just... it's just when it's in *my* hand that I feel like I'm gonna lose control. If I don't have to touch it, the panic isn't really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor is because you feel like you might lose control of it when it's in your hand?\n\nPatient: Yeah, losing control... I think that's exactly it. I mean, I know it’s just a tool I trained with. I can use my clippers all day long without even thinking about it. I actually just upgraded to these new cordless Wahl clippers, and the battery life on them is unbelievable. I used to have the corded ones, and the thick cord would always get tangled around the hydraulic pump on the barber chair. One time, it got so wrapped up I actually tripped and almost took my client down with me. He was a really good sport about it, thankfully. He’s this guy who owns a bakery down the street, and he always brings in these amazing cheese danishes when he comes in for a fade. I don't even have much of a sweet tooth, usually. I'm more of a savory guy, you know? Give me a good steak over a piece of cake any day. But man, those danishes. They remind me of how my mom used to bake when I was a kid. She made this incredible banana bread with chocolate chips in it. We had this massive walnut tree in our backyard, and we'd spend hours in the fall just cracking walnuts on the patio to put in the bread. My hands would get stained completely brown from the husks. It took days to scrub it off. \n\nBut, uh, anyway. Sorry. What were we talking about? Oh, the razor. Yeah, like I was saying, I can use clippers all day, but when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station... it feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. Just having it sitting there open makes me feel like something terrible is guaranteed to happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor is more about hurting yourself or others when you're holding it?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if it's one more than the other, honestly. It's just the thought of the blade slipping and doing damage. Like, the idea of a deep cut... My roommate actually sliced his finger open pretty bad just last week chopping onions. He thinks he's this master chef because he watches all those cooking competitions on Netflix. You know the ones where they have to make a gourmet meal out of, like, gummy bears and a can of tuna? He binge-watches those and then tries to do all this rapid-fire knife work in our tiny apartment kitchen. It's a total disaster. The smoke alarm goes off almost every time he cooks. Our landlord actually came banging on the door a few weeks ago because he thought the building was on fire. \n\nThe landlord is this older guy, Mr. Henderson, and he's always wearing these vintage velour tracksuits. Like, full matching sets, burgundy and navy blue, straight out of the 90s. He told us he used to be a breakdancer back in the day, which I just cannot picture at all because he walks with this heavy wooden cane now. He said he blew his knee out doing a backflip off a speaker at a block party in Queens. He's got all these crazy stories about New York in the 80s. \n\nBut, um... sorry, I lost my train of thought again. Hurting myself or others? Yeah, I really couldn't tell you which is worse or if one scares me more. I don't think it's specifically one or the other. It's just this overwhelming feeling that if I'm holding it, a disaster is going to happen, no matter who ends up bleeding.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Shaving myself? No, no way. If it's in my hand, it doesn't matter who the blade is touching, the panic is exactly the same. I actually tried it once, thinking maybe if I just practiced on my own cheek, I could get over it. But I couldn't even bring it up to my face. My hand was shaking so bad, I just put it down and used my electric shaver instead. 

I've had that electric shaver for years, actually. It's a Braun. My grandpa gave it to me for my high school graduation. He was always really big on being clean-shaven. He was in the Navy, so he had all these strict grooming habits. He used to shine his shoes every single Sunday night while watching 60 Minutes. The smell of shoe polish always makes me think of him and the ticking sound of that stopwatch on the TV screen. He had this massive collection of nautical clocks, too. They chimed every half hour, and it drove my grandma crazy. She ended up taking the batteries out of half of them when he wasn't looking. She was a character. She used to knit these incredibly itchy wool sweaters for all of us every Christmas. I had one that was bright orange with a weird green reindeer on it. I had to wear it for the family photo one year, and I looked like a traffic cone. 

But, uh, yeah. Using it on myself? Absolutely not. It doesn't make a difference. If I'm holding it, I just can't do it.

A squirrel ended up moving into it instead of a bird, though. It chewed the hole bigger and just took over. Squirrels are relentless, man. They get into the bird feeders at my parents' house, and my dad has tried every trick in the book to keep them out. He even put hot sauce on the seeds once, but I think they actually liked it. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, sorry. If Marcus is holding his razor, or anyone else is, I'm fine. I can stand right next to him and talk to him while he's using it. It's just... it's just when it's in *my* hand that I feel like I'm gonna lose control. If I don't have to touch it, the panic isn't really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor is because you feel like you might lose control of it when it's in your hand?\n\nPatient: Yeah, losing control... I think that's exactly it. I mean, I know it’s just a tool I trained with. I can use my clippers all day long without even thinking about it. I used to have the corded ones, and the thick cord would always get tangled around the hydraulic pump on the barber chair. One time, it got so wrapped up I actually tripped and almost took my client down with me. He was a really good sport about it, thankfully. He’s this guy who owns a bakery down the street, and he always brings in these amazing cheese danishes when he comes in for a fade. I don't even have much of a sweet tooth, usually. I'm more of a savory guy, you know? Give me a good steak over a piece of cake any day. But man, those danishes. They remind me of how my mom used to bake when I was a kid. She made this incredible banana bread with chocolate chips in it. We had this massive walnut tree in our backyard, and we'd spend hours in the fall just cracking walnuts on the patio to put in the bread. My hands would get stained completely brown from the husks. Yeah, like I was saying, I can use clippers all day, but when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station... it feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. It's just the thought of the blade slipping and doing damage. Like, the idea of a deep cut... My roommate actually sliced his finger open pretty bad just last week chopping onions. He binge-watches those and then tries to do all this rapid-fire knife work in our tiny apartment kitchen. It's a total disaster. The smoke alarm goes off almost every time he cooks. My grandpa gave it to me for my high school graduation. He was always really big on being clean-shaven. He was in the Navy, so he had all these strict grooming habits. He had this massive collection of nautical clocks, too. They chimed every half hour, and it drove my grandma crazy. She ended up taking the batteries out of half of them when he wasn't looking. She was a character. She used to knit these incredibly itchy wool sweaters for all of us every Christmas. I had one that was bright orange with a weird green reindeer on it. I had to wear it for the family photo one year, and I looked like a traffic cone. \n\nBut, uh, yeah. Using it on myself? Absolutely not. It doesn't make a difference. If I'm holding it, I just can't do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be less afraid of the straight razor if you felt completely in control of your movements while holding it?\n\nPatient: If I felt completely in control? It's not even a rational thought process anymore, you know?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Confident in my ability? I mean, I guess I'm pretty confident with everything else I do at the shop. Fades, scissor work, all that. But the straight razor... It's getting really bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any episodes of intense fear or discomfort that reach a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: A peak within minutes? Uh... no, not really. It's not like it just hits me out of nowhere like that. It's just... when I actually have to do the shave, I'm just terrified the entire time, from start to finish. It feels like it takes forever. But I swear it feels like hours. \n\nI actually bought this vintage wall clock for the shop recently to help me keep track of my appointments better. That's the thing. I know it’s just a tool, right? I mean, I trained with it for hours and hours in barber school. We had this instructor, Mr. Henderson, who was an absolute stickler for technique. He used to make us practice on balloons covered in shaving cream. If you popped the balloon, you failed for the day. I swear, the sound of a balloon popping in that room sounded like a gunshot. It was so echoey in there. The building was this old converted warehouse, high ceilings, exposed ductwork... freezing in the winter, honestly. We all had to wear these thick wool sweaters under our smocks. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I can use clippers all day long. Buzz cuts, fades, whatever, no problem. But when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station, I just... I get this overwhelming feeling that I’m about to hurt somebody. Or like I'm going to slip and cut myself really badly. It's just sitting there, and I can't stop thinking about the blade.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of harming someone or making a mistake limited to when you're using the straight razor, or do you worry about it with other tools or situations at work as well?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the straight razor. I mean, I use scissors all day, clippers, trimmers... none of that bothers me. I can do a skin fade in my sleep. Actually, I had this guy come in last week who wanted this super intricate design shaved into the side of his head—like a geometric pattern thing. It took me almost an hour just with the trimmers. He was a graphic designer or something, worked for this start-up downtown that makes an app for... I don't even know, dog walking? He showed me the app, it had this little cartoon corgi as the logo. My sister used to have a corgi, named him Waffles. Cutest dog ever, but he shed everywhere. You'd find dog hair in your food, in your shoes... she ended up having to buy this crazy expensive vacuum cleaner just to keep up with it. One of those ones that looks like a spaceship, you know? \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't worry about hurting anyone with the scissors or the clippers. It's literally just the straight razor. When it's just laying there open, that's when my brain goes to that dark place.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of using the straight razor affecting other parts of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't really encounter straight razors anywhere else, you know? Unless I'm at an antique store or something, but I don't really go to those. My girlfriend loves them, though. We went to this huge antique mall last weekend, the kind that’s in an old converted barn? It smelled like dust and old books. She spent like two hours looking at these vintage Pyrex bowls. The ones with the little flowers on the side, you know? Apparently, they're super collectible now. Some of them go for hundreds of dollars online, which is crazy to me because my grandma used to have the exact same ones and she just kept leftover meatloaf in them. My grandma made the best meatloaf, too. She put a little bit of brown sugar in the ketchup glaze on top. It sounds weird, but it caramelized in the oven and it was incredible. She used to serve it with these mashed potatoes that had way too much butter in them, but nobody complained. \n\nBut, uh, no, the razor thing doesn't really bother me at home or anything. Just at the shop when I have to use it.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social situations or activities outside of work because of your fear of straight razors?\n\nPatient: No, no, I still go out and do stuff. Like I said, I don't really run into straight razors at a bar or a movie theater, right? So it doesn't stop me from seeing my friends or my girlfriend. Actually, me and my buddies are supposed to go to this axe-throwing place this weekend. Have you ever done that? It's wild. You stand in these little cages and just chuck a hatchet at a wooden target. First time I went, I was terrible at it. The axe kept bouncing off the wood and flying back at me. The instructor said it was all in the wrist, like skipping a rock. I used to be so good at skipping rocks when I was a kid. We lived near this lake, Lake Winnebago, and my dad would take me out there on Sunday mornings. The water was always so still, like glass. He taught me how to find the perfect flat stone. You want one that's smooth, but not too heavy. My dad had this crazy record, he could get a rock to skip like twelve times. I think my best was maybe seven. He always wore this ridiculous bucket hat when we went to the lake, covered in fishing lures. He thought it was hilarious, but my mom hated it. She actually tried to throw it away once, but he fished it out of the trash... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, social stuff is fine. I'm not avoiding going out or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of the straight razor related to the fact that it's used for shaving, or would you be just as afraid of it if you were using it for something else?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure, honestly. I've never really thought about using it for anything else. I mean, it's a straight razor, so shaving is pretty much all it's good for, right? Well, I guess you could cut stuff with it, like opening boxes or something. Speaking of boxes, we got this massive shipment of new pomades at the shop yesterday. The boxes were huge, and they were packed with all these little styrofoam peanuts. I hate those things. They stick to everything because of the static electricity. You try to throw them away and they just cling to your hand, your shirt, the trash can... it's a nightmare. We had to spend twenty minutes just sweeping up all the packing peanuts before we could even unpack the product. The pomade is pretty nice, though. It's got this sort of matte finish, smells like sandalwood and... maybe a little bit of bergamot? My old roommate used to burn this sandalwood incense all the time. He was super into meditation and yoga and all that. He had this whole shrine set up in the corner of the living room with crystals and singing bowls. He tried to teach me how to meditate once, but I just couldn't clear my mind, you know? I kept thinking about what I was gonna have for dinner. I think we ordered Thai food that night. Pad Thai, extra spicy... \n\nBut, uh, to answer your question, I don't know if it's just the shaving part. I think it's just the razor itself. Just the way it looks when it's open.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel afraid of the straight razor if you were using it for a task that didn't involve shaving?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think so. Even if I was just using it to open one of those boxes I was talking about, it wouldn't matter. It's just... I know it’s just a tool, you know? I went to school for this, I trained with it, and I can use clippers and shears all day long without batting an eye. But when that straight razor is just sitting there, open at my station... man, it just feels like I’m about to hurt somebody, or like I'm gonna slip and cut myself really badly. It's crazy because I've never even nicked anyone before. Well, except this one time in barber college, but that was with scissors, not a razor. We were practicing on these mannequin heads first, right? They had real human hair, which was always a little creepy to me. I always wondered where they got the hair from. Like, do people donate it specifically for mannequins, or is it just leftover from wig makers? Anyway, my buddy in class, Steve, he accidentally cut off a chunk of his own thumb while texturizing the mannequin's hair. Bled everywhere. The instructor had to wrap it up in this huge bandage, and Steve couldn't cut hair for a week. He ended up dropping out a month later to go work at his uncle's auto body shop. I saw him a couple of years ago at a gas station, he looked good. He was driving this vintage Mustang that he restored himself. Cherry red, white leather interior... beautiful car. I've always wanted a classic car, maybe a '69 Charger. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, it doesn't matter what I'm using the razor for. Just seeing it open like that, it just... it terrifies me that someone's gonna get hurt.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be afraid of the straight razor if it were blunt and couldn't cut anyone?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? If it was completely blunt and couldn't cut anything... I guess it wouldn't be as bad, but it would still look the same, you know? Just that shape of it. It's funny you mention blunt things, because my mom used to have these butter knives that were so old and worn down they couldn't even cut hot butter. We had this big Sunday breakfast tradition at my house growing up. Pancakes, bacon, the whole nine yards. My dad made the best pancakes. He'd put chocolate chips in them, but only on my brother's side of the griddle, because I hated chocolate chips in my pancakes. I liked blueberries. Fresh ones, not the frozen kind that turn the whole pancake purple. We used to go berry picking every summer at this farm upstate. You'd get these little green baskets and just fill them up. My brother would eat half of what he picked before we even got to the weigh station. The farmer always knew, but he never said anything. He was this old guy with a huge white beard, looked exactly like Santa Claus in overalls. He had a tractor that he let us sit on once. It was a John Deere, bright green. I always thought tractors were so cool when I was a kid. I wanted to be a farmer for like a year, until I realized how early they have to wake up. I am definitely not a morning person. Even now, getting to the barbershop by 8 AM is a struggle. I usually need at least two cups of coffee before I can even hold a conversation. \n\nBut, um, what were we talking about? Oh, the blunt razor. Yeah, I don't know. I think just seeing it open would still give me that panic, even if I knew it was dull.\n\nDoctor: Would your fear of the straight razor change if you were using it for a task that is very similar to shaving but not actually shaving, such as shaving cream off a balloon like you did in training?\n\nPatient: Shaving a balloon... man, I haven't thought about that since barber college. We used to go through so many balloons. Just those cheap ones from the dollar store. My instructor, Mr. Davis, he'd blow them up so tight that if you even breathed wrong with the blade, they'd pop and shaving cream would go everywhere. I actually hate the smell of that cheap foam shaving cream now. It reminds me of my grandpa, though. He used to use it every morning. He had this little badger hair brush and a ceramic mug. He was a mechanic, so his hands were always covered in grease, but his face was always perfectly smooth. He worked on airplanes, actually. Commercial jets. We got to go to the hangar once when I was like ten. It's crazy how massive those planes are up close. You don't realize it when you're sitting inside them, eating those tiny bags of pretzels. I always ask for the Biscoff cookies instead, those things are amazing with coffee. I tried to buy a box of them at the grocery store once, but they just didn't taste the same on the ground. Maybe it's the altitude? I read somewhere that your taste buds get numb when you're flying. \n\nBut, um... wait, the balloon thing. Honestly, I'm not really sure if it would change anything. I don't think it would. Because I'd still be holding the razor, and it would still be open in my hand. So yeah, I don't know if what I'm shaving actually makes a difference to the panic.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor would decrease if you were using it in a situation where there was no possibility of harming anyone?\n\nPatient: I don't know, honestly. Like if I was alone in a room with it? I guess... I mean, I'd still be holding it, right? And I'd still be the one holding it, so I could still slip and cut myself. My hands get so sweaty when I even think about it. I have to use that talcum powder we have at the shop just to dry them off sometimes. We use this specific brand, Clubman Pinaud. It has that classic barbershop smell, you know? Sort of powdery and masculine. My dad used to wear a cologne that smelled just like it. Old Spice, I think. Or maybe Brut? He always got those gift sets for Christmas with the aftershave and the deodorant. I still buy him one every year, just out of habit. He's retired now, lives down in Florida. Plays golf every single day. I don't get the appeal of golf, honestly. It just seems like a lot of walking in the hot sun to hit a tiny ball. I'd rather be fishing. We used to go fishing on this lake when I was a teenager. Caught a massive largemouth bass once, must have been five pounds. \n\nBut, uh, what were you asking? Oh, if nobody could get hurt. I don't know, I just don't think there's any situation where nobody could get hurt if I'm holding an open razor. I'm there, so I could get hurt. It's just the razor itself, I think.\n\nDoctor: Would your fear of the straight razor be less intense if you were to use it for shaving a mannequin or a dummy, rather than a real person?\n\nPatient: A mannequin? Man, I haven't seen one of those since barber college either. We had these heads we called \"pivot points,\" they had this weirdly realistic hair that you could actually cut and style. Some guys would get so attached to their mannequins, they'd name them and talk to them while they were cutting. There was this one guy, Tommy, he named his \"Brenda\" and he'd actually get mad if someone else touched her. Tommy was a trip. He ended up dropping out and moving to Vegas to become a dealer at one of the casinos. Blackjack, I think. I went to Vegas once for my 21st birthday. We stayed at the Luxor, you know, the giant pyramid? It was so confusing inside, the elevators go up at an angle. We got so lost trying to find our room the first night. We ended up at this buffet at like 3 AM eating prime rib and crab legs. The crab legs were okay, but I've had way better in Maryland. My aunt lives in Annapolis, and she makes this crab dip that is just unbelievable. She puts Old Bay seasoning in everything. She even puts it on popcorn, which sounds gross, but it's actually really good if you have a cold beer with it. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the dummy. I don't know, honestly. Probably not. Because, like I was saying, it's just the razor itself. It doesn't really matter what's in front of me, real person or plastic head. If I'm holding that blade open, I'm just terrified I'm going to slip and slice my own hand open or something. So no, I don't think it would be any less intense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor would decrease if someone else was holding it, but you were still in the room?\n\nPatient: If someone else was holding it... yeah, I think so. If I'm not the one holding it, it's not as bad. Like, the other guys at the shop use them all the time. My buddy Marcus, he's in the chair next to mine, he does these incredibly sharp line-ups with his. He's got this custom-made handle, it's like a dark walnut wood with his initials engraved on it. He paid a fortune for it from this guy on Instagram who makes them by hand. Instagram is crazy for stuff like that now. You can find people making anything. I follow this one account where a guy just restores old axes. He finds them rusted in barns and polishes them up until they look brand new. It's weirdly relaxing to watch. He uses this special grinding wheel, and the sparks fly everywhere. Reminds me of my high school shop class. We had to make a birdhouse, which was so cliché, but I actually tried to make mine look like a miniature log cabin. I spent weeks cutting little dowels for the logs. A squirrel ended up moving into it instead of a bird, though. It chewed the hole bigger and just took over. Squirrels are relentless, man. They get into the bird feeders at my parents' house, and my dad has tried every trick in the book to keep them out. He even put hot sauce on the seeds once, but I think they actually liked it. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, sorry. If Marcus is holding his razor, or anyone else is, I'm fine. I can stand right next to him and talk to him while he's using it. It's just... it's just when it's in *my* hand that I feel like I'm gonna lose control. If I don't have to touch it, the panic isn't really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor is because you feel like you might lose control of it when it's in your hand?\n\nPatient: Yeah, losing control... I think that's exactly it. I mean, I know it’s just a tool I trained with. I can use my clippers all day long without even thinking about it. I actually just upgraded to these new cordless Wahl clippers, and the battery life on them is unbelievable. Yeah, like I was saying, I can use clippers all day, but when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station... it feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. Just having it sitting there open makes me feel like something terrible is guaranteed to happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor is more about hurting yourself or others when you're holding it?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if it's one more than the other, honestly. He binge-watches those and then tries to do all this rapid-fire knife work in our tiny apartment kitchen. It's a total disaster. The smoke alarm goes off almost every time he cooks. He was a mechanic, so his hands were always covered in grease, but his face was always perfectly smooth. He worked on airplanes, actually. Commercial jets. We got to go to the hangar once when I was like ten. It's crazy how massive those planes are up close. You don't realize it when you're sitting inside them, eating those tiny bags of pretzels. I always ask for the Biscoff cookies instead, those things are amazing with coffee. I tried to buy a box of them at the grocery store once, but they just didn't taste the same on the ground. Maybe it's the altitude? I read somewhere that your taste buds get numb when you're flying. \n\nBut, um... wait, the balloon thing. Honestly, I'm not really sure if it would change anything. I don't think it would. Because I'd still be holding the razor, and it would still be open in my hand. So yeah, I don't know if what I'm shaving actually makes a difference to the panic.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor would decrease if you were using it in a situation where there was no possibility of harming anyone?\n\nPatient: I don't know, honestly. Like if I was alone in a room with it? I guess... I mean, I'd still be holding it, right? And I'd still be the one holding it, so I could still slip and cut myself. My hands get so sweaty when I even think about it. I have to use that talcum powder we have at the shop just to dry them off sometimes. We use this specific brand, Clubman Pinaud. It has that classic barbershop smell, you know? Sort of powdery and masculine. My dad used to wear a cologne that smelled just like it. Old Spice, I think. Or maybe Brut? He always got those gift sets for Christmas with the aftershave and the deodorant. I still buy him one every year, just out of habit. He's retired now, lives down in Florida. Plays golf every single day. I don't get the appeal of golf, honestly. It just seems like a lot of walking in the hot sun to hit a tiny ball. I'd rather be fishing. We used to go fishing on this lake when I was a teenager. Caught a massive largemouth bass once, must have been five pounds. \n\nBut, uh, what were you asking? Oh, if nobody could get hurt. I don't know, I just don't think there's any situation where nobody could get hurt if I'm holding an open razor. I'm there, so I could get hurt. It's just the razor itself, I think.\n\nDoctor: Would your fear of the straight razor be less intense if you were to use it for shaving a mannequin or a dummy, rather than a real person?\n\nPatient: A mannequin? Man, I haven't seen one of those since barber college either. We had these heads we called \"pivot points,\" they had this weirdly realistic hair that you could actually cut and style. Some guys would get so attached to their mannequins, they'd name them and talk to them while they were cutting. There was this one guy, Tommy, he named his \"Brenda\" and he'd actually get mad if someone else touched her. Tommy was a trip. He ended up dropping out and moving to Vegas to become a dealer at one of the casinos. Blackjack, I think. I went to Vegas once for my 21st birthday. We stayed at the Luxor, you know, the giant pyramid? It was so confusing inside, the elevators go up at an angle. We got so lost trying to find our room the first night. We ended up at this buffet at like 3 AM eating prime rib and crab legs. The crab legs were okay, but I've had way better in Maryland. My aunt lives in Annapolis, and she makes this crab dip that is just unbelievable. She puts Old Bay seasoning in everything. She even puts it on popcorn, which sounds gross, but it's actually really good if you have a cold beer with it. \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the dummy. I don't know, honestly. Probably not. Because, like I was saying, it's just the razor itself. It doesn't really matter what's in front of me, real person or plastic head. If I'm holding that blade open, I'm just terrified I'm going to slip and slice my own hand open or something. So no, I don't think it would be any less intense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor would decrease if someone else was holding it, but you were still in the room?\n\nPatient: If someone else was holding it... yeah, I think so. If I'm not the one holding it, it's not as bad. Like, the other guys at the shop use them all the time. My buddy Marcus, he's in the chair next to mine, he does these incredibly sharp line-ups with his. He's got this custom-made handle, it's like a dark walnut wood with his initials engraved on it. He paid a fortune for it from this guy on Instagram who makes them by hand. Instagram is crazy for stuff like that now. You can find people making anything. I follow this one account where a guy just restores old axes. He finds them rusted in barns and polishes them up until they look brand new. It's weirdly relaxing to watch. He uses this special grinding wheel, and the sparks fly everywhere. Reminds me of my high school shop class. We had to make a birdhouse, which was so cliché, but I actually tried to make mine look like a miniature log cabin. I spent weeks cutting little dowels for the logs. A squirrel ended up moving into it instead of a bird, though. It chewed the hole bigger and just took over. Squirrels are relentless, man. They get into the bird feeders at my parents' house, and my dad has tried every trick in the book to keep them out. He even put hot sauce on the seeds once, but I think they actually liked it. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, sorry. If Marcus is holding his razor, or anyone else is, I'm fine. I can stand right next to him and talk to him while he's using it. It's just... it's just when it's in *my* hand that I feel like I'm gonna lose control. If I don't have to touch it, the panic isn't really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor is because you feel like you might lose control of it when it's in your hand?\n\nPatient: Yeah, losing control... I think that's exactly it. I mean, I know it’s just a tool I trained with. I can use my clippers all day long without even thinking about it. I actually just upgraded to these new cordless Wahl clippers, and the battery life on them is unbelievable. I used to have the corded ones, and the thick cord would always get tangled around the hydraulic pump on the barber chair. One time, it got so wrapped up I actually tripped and almost took my client down with me. He was a really good sport about it, thankfully. He’s this guy who owns a bakery down the street, and he always brings in these amazing cheese danishes when he comes in for a fade. I don't even have much of a sweet tooth, usually. I'm more of a savory guy, you know? Give me a good steak over a piece of cake any day. But man, those danishes. They remind me of how my mom used to bake when I was a kid. She made this incredible banana bread with chocolate chips in it. We had this massive walnut tree in our backyard, and we'd spend hours in the fall just cracking walnuts on the patio to put in the bread. My hands would get stained completely brown from the husks. It took days to scrub it off. \n\nBut, uh, anyway. Sorry. What were we talking about? Oh, the razor. Yeah, like I was saying, I can use clippers all day, but when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station... it feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. Just having it sitting there open makes me feel like something terrible is guaranteed to happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor is more about hurting yourself or others when you're holding it?\n\nPatient: I don't really know if it's one more than the other, honestly. It's just the thought of the blade slipping and doing damage. Like, the idea of a deep cut... My roommate actually sliced his finger open pretty bad just last week chopping onions. He thinks he's this master chef because he watches all those cooking competitions on Netflix. You know the ones where they have to make a gourmet meal out of, like, gummy bears and a can of tuna? He binge-watches those and then tries to do all this rapid-fire knife work in our tiny apartment kitchen. It's a total disaster. The smoke alarm goes off almost every time he cooks. Our landlord actually came banging on the door a few weeks ago because he thought the building was on fire. \n\nThe landlord is this older guy, Mr. Henderson, and he's always wearing these vintage velour tracksuits. Like, full matching sets, burgundy and navy blue, straight out of the 90s. He told us he used to be a breakdancer back in the day, which I just cannot picture at all because he walks with this heavy wooden cane now. He said he blew his knee out doing a backflip off a speaker at a block party in Queens. He's got all these crazy stories about New York in the 80s. \n\nBut, um... sorry, I lost my train of thought again. Hurting myself or others? Yeah, I really couldn't tell you which is worse or if one scares me more. I don't think it's specifically one or the other. It's just this overwhelming feeling that if I'm holding it, a disaster is going to happen, no matter who ends up bleeding.\n\nDoctor: Would your fear of the straight razor decrease if you were using it for shaving yourself?\n\nPatient: Shaving myself? No, no way. If it's in my hand, it doesn't matter who the blade is touching, the panic is exactly the same. I actually tried it once, thinking maybe if I just practiced on my own cheek, I could get over it. But I couldn't even bring it up to my face. My hand was shaking so bad, I just put it down and used my electric shaver instead. \n\nI've had that electric shaver for years, actually. It's a Braun. My grandpa gave it to me for my high school graduation. He was always really big on being clean-shaven. He was in the Navy, so he had all these strict grooming habits. He used to shine his shoes every single Sunday night while watching 60 Minutes. The smell of shoe polish always makes me think of him and the ticking sound of that stopwatch on the TV screen. He had this massive collection of nautical clocks, too. They chimed every half hour, and it drove my grandma crazy. She ended up taking the batteries out of half of them when he wasn't looking. She was a character. She used to knit these incredibly itchy wool sweaters for all of us every Christmas. I had one that was bright orange with a weird green reindeer on it. I had to wear it for the family photo one year, and I looked like a traffic cone. \n\nBut, uh, yeah. Using it on myself? Absolutely not. It doesn't make a difference. If I'm holding it, I just can't do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be less afraid of the straight razor if you felt completely in control of your movements while holding it?\n\nPatient: If I felt completely in control? I mean... maybe? I don't know. It's hard to even imagine feeling in control with it at this point. I guess if I knew for a fact I wouldn't slip, it might be better. But my hands just get so clammy. I actually use this special talcum powder at the shop to keep my hands dry. It smells like sandalwood. It's the same brand my old mentor, Sal, used to use. \n\nSal was a legend. He had his shop for, like, forty years. He had this old-school cash register that actually went *ding* when you opened it, and it was so heavy it took three of us to move it when he finally retired and sold the place. He moved down to Florida, somewhere near Boca Raton, I think. He sends me postcards sometimes. Always with flamingos or alligators on them. He said he took up golf but he's terrible at it, keeps losing balls in the water hazards. He actually saw an iguana the size of a dog just sunning itself on the 9th hole once. I can't even imagine living somewhere with giant lizards just running around like squirrels. I hate reptiles. I went to a petting zoo once when I was a kid and they put this huge yellow python around my neck for a photo... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Sorry, I got totally sidetracked. Um, being in control? Yeah, I don't know if feeling in control would fix it, because the fear just hits me the second I see the blade. It's not even a rational thought process anymore, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of the straight razor would decrease if you felt completely confident in your ability to handle it safely?\n\nPatient: Confident in my ability? I mean, I guess I'm pretty confident with everything else I do at the shop. Fades, scissor work, all that. But the straight razor... I don't know if confidence would even matter. It's just the blade itself. It's so sharp. It reminds me of this Japanese chef's knife my brother bought last year. He spent like four hundred dollars on it. It came in this beautiful wooden box with a velvet lining. He's obsessed with Japanese culture. He actually went to Kyoto for two weeks and came back talking about how he wanted to learn how to make traditional swords. He even bought a bunch of books on metallurgy. He's always picking up these intense hobbies and dropping them a month later. Like the time he bought a full home brewing kit and turned his basement into a brewery. The whole house smelled like yeast for a month, and the beer tasted awful. It was like drinking carbonated bread. My mom was so mad because he spilled a whole vat of it on her vintage rug. She got that rug at an estate sale from this lady who used to be an opera singer... \n\nWait, sorry, what was the question? Confident in handling the razor? Yeah, honestly, I don't think it would make a difference. The second I even look at it, my heart starts racing. It doesn't matter how much I know I *should* be able to handle it safely, the fear just overrides everything else.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Something other than shaving? Like... what, cutting a box open or something? I don't know, honestly. I don't think so. A blade is a blade. It's just... the way it looks, the way it folds out of the handle. It reminds me of this pocket knife my dad used to carry when we went camping. He had this old Swiss Army knife, the red one with the little toothpick and tweezers that always got lost. We used to go up to the Adirondacks every summer. We'd rent this cabin right on the lake, and the water was always freezing, even in August. My dad would try to teach me how to whittle sticks with that knife, making little spears for roasting marshmallows. We'd make s'mores every night until we felt sick. One time, my sister dropped her marshmallow in the dirt and cried for an hour because it was the last one in the bag. She was always so dramatic. She's an actor now, actually, living in LA. Oh, right, the razor. Yeah, no, I don't think it matters what I'm using it for. Shaving, cutting string, whatever. If I have to open that blade and hold it, I just... I can't. The panic is just too much.

Actually, getting them into the shop was a total nightmare, my buddy threw his back out just trying to get them over the curb... but anyway, I'm here because... well, I’ve gotten to the point where I’ll do almost anything to avoid using the straight razor at work. \n\nI'll ask the other guys to switch clients with me, or I'll just tell the customer the blade’s missing, or I'll literally hide the thing in my drawer. And it sucks because, like, this one regular, Tom, he always brings me coffee from the place next door—black, two sugars—and he always expects the full hot towel shave. If I can’t get out of it and I actually have to do it, I’m just... I'm shaking through the entire shave. It's getting really bad.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any episodes of intense fear or discomfort that reach a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: A peak within minutes? Uh... no, not really. It's not like it just hits me out of nowhere like that. It's just... when I actually have to do the shave, I'm just terrified the entire time, from start to finish. It feels like it takes forever. A good hot towel shave usually takes about, what, fifteen minutes? But I swear it feels like hours. \n\nI actually bought this vintage wall clock for the shop recently to help me keep track of my appointments better. Found it at this massive flea market out by the highway. It's got this really heavy brass pendulum, looks amazing against the brick wall in the shop. The guy wanted fifty bucks for it, but I talked him down to thirty. I've always been pretty good at haggling. My dad taught me how to do it when we used to go to those classic car shows when I was a kid... he had this '68 Mustang that he was always buying parts for, so we were always negotiating with people out of the trunks of their cars. \n\nBut, uh... wait, sorry, what did you ask me? Something about minutes? Yeah, no, I don't get those sudden spikes of fear you're talking about. Just the shaking when I have the razor.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of using the straight razor at work related to a concern about harming someone or making a mistake?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. That's the thing. I know it’s just a tool, right? I mean, I trained with it for hours and hours in barber school. We had this instructor, Mr. Henderson, who was an absolute stickler for technique. He used to make us practice on balloons covered in shaving cream. If you popped the balloon, you failed for the day. I swear, the sound of a balloon popping in that room sounded like a gunshot. It was so echoey in there. The building was this old converted warehouse, high ceilings, exposed ductwork... freezing in the winter, honestly. We all had to wear these thick wool sweaters under our smocks. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I can use clippers all day long. Buzz cuts, fades, whatever, no problem. But when that straight razor is just sitting open at my station, I just... I get this overwhelming feeling that I’m about to hurt somebody. Or like I'm going to slip and cut myself really badly. It's just sitting there, and I can't stop thinking about the blade.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of harming someone or making a mistake limited to when you're using the straight razor, or do you worry about it with other tools or situations at work as well?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just the straight razor. I mean, I use scissors all day, clippers, trimmers... none of that bothers me. I can do a skin fade in my sleep. Actually, I had this guy come in last week who wanted this super intricate design shaved into the side of his head—like a geometric pattern thing. It took me almost an hour just with the trimmers. He was a graphic designer or something, worked for this start-up downtown that makes an app for... I don't even know, dog walking? He showed me the app, it had this little cartoon corgi as the logo. My sister used to have a corgi, named him Waffles. Cutest dog ever, but he shed everywhere. You'd find dog hair in your food, in your shoes... she ended up having to buy this crazy expensive vacuum cleaner just to keep up with it. One of those ones that looks like a spaceship, you know? \n\nBut yeah, no, I don't worry about hurting anyone with the scissors or the clippers. It's literally just the straight razor. When it's just laying there open, that's when my brain goes to that dark place.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of using the straight razor affecting other parts of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't really encounter straight razors anywhere else, you know? Unless I'm at an antique store or something, but I don't really go to those. My girlfriend loves them, though. We went to this huge antique mall last weekend, the kind that’s in an old converted barn? It smelled like dust and old books. She spent like two hours looking at these vintage Pyrex bowls. The ones with the little flowers on the side, you know? Apparently, they're super collectible now. Some of them go for hundreds of dollars online, which is crazy to me because my grandma used to have the exact same ones and she just kept leftover meatloaf in them. My grandma made the best meatloaf, too. She put a little bit of brown sugar in the ketchup glaze on top. It sounds weird, but it caramelized in the oven and it was incredible. She used to serve it with these mashed potatoes that had way too much butter in them, but nobody complained. \n\nBut, uh, no, the razor thing doesn't really bother me at home or anything. Just at the shop when I have to use it.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social situations or activities outside of work because of your fear of straight razors?\n\nPatient: No, no, I still go out and do stuff. Like I said, I don't really run into straight razors at a bar or a movie theater, right? So it doesn't stop me from seeing my friends or my girlfriend. Actually, me and my buddies are supposed to go to this axe-throwing place this weekend. Have you ever done that? It's wild. You stand in these little cages and just chuck a hatchet at a wooden target. First time I went, I was terrible at it. The axe kept bouncing off the wood and flying back at me. The instructor said it was all in the wrist, like skipping a rock. I used to be so good at skipping rocks when I was a kid. We lived near this lake, Lake Winnebago, and my dad would take me out there on Sunday mornings. The water was always so still, like glass. He taught me how to find the perfect flat stone. You want one that's smooth, but not too heavy. My dad had this crazy record, he could get a rock to skip like twelve times. I think my best was maybe seven. He always wore this ridiculous bucket hat when we went to the lake, covered in fishing lures. He thought it was hilarious, but my mom hated it. She actually tried to throw it away once, but he fished it out of the trash... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, social stuff is fine. I'm not avoiding going out or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of the straight razor related to the fact that it's used for shaving, or would you be just as afraid of it if you were using it for something else?\n\nPatient: Um... Well, I guess you could cut stuff with it, like opening boxes or something. Speaking of boxes, we got this massive shipment of new pomades at the shop yesterday. The boxes were huge, and they were packed with all these little styrofoam peanuts. I hate those things. They stick to everything because of the static electricity. You try to throw them away and they just cling to your hand, your shirt, the trash can... it's a nightmare. We had to spend twenty minutes just sweeping up all the packing peanuts before we could even unpack the product. The pomade is pretty nice, though. It's got this sort of matte finish, smells like sandalwood and... maybe a little bit of bergamot? My old roommate used to burn this sandalwood incense all the time. He was super into meditation and yoga and all that. He had this whole shrine set up in the corner of the living room with crystals and singing bowls. He tried to teach me how to meditate once, but I just couldn't clear my mind, you know? I kept thinking about what I was gonna have for dinner. I think we ordered Thai food that night. I went to school for this, I trained with it, and I can use clippers and shears all day long without batting an eye. But when that straight razor is just sitting there, open at my station... man, it just feels like I’m about to hurt somebody, or like I'm gonna slip and cut myself really badly. Like, do people donate it specifically for mannequins, or is it just leftover from wig makers? Anyway, my buddy in class, Steve, he accidentally cut off a chunk of his own thumb while texturizing the mannequin's hair. Bled everywhere. The instructor had to wrap it up in this huge bandage, and Steve couldn't cut hair for a week. He ended up dropping out a month later to go work at his uncle's auto body shop. I saw him a couple of years ago at a gas station, he looked good. He was driving this vintage Mustang that he restored himself. Cherry red, white leather interior... beautiful car. I've always wanted a classic car, maybe a '69 Charger. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, it doesn't matter what I'm using the razor for. Just seeing it open like that, it just... it terrifies me that someone's gonna get hurt.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be afraid of the straight razor if it were blunt and couldn't cut anyone?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? If it was completely blunt and couldn't cut anything... I guess it wouldn't be as bad, but it would still look the same, you know? Just that shape of it. It's funny you mention blunt things, because my mom used to have these butter knives that were so old and worn down they couldn't even cut hot butter. We had this big Sunday breakfast tradition at my house growing up. Pancakes, bacon, the whole nine yards. My dad made the best pancakes. He'd put chocolate chips in them, but only on my brother's side of the griddle, because I hated chocolate chips in my pancakes. I liked blueberries. Fresh ones, not the frozen kind that turn the whole pancake purple. We used to go berry picking every summer at this farm upstate. You'd get these little green baskets and just fill them up. My brother would eat half of what he picked before we even got to the weigh station. The farmer always knew, but he never said anything. He was this old guy with a huge white beard, looked exactly like Santa Claus in overalls. He had a tractor that he let us sit on once. It was a John Deere, bright green. I always thought tractors were so cool when I was a kid. I wanted to be a farmer for like a year, until I realized how early they have to wake up. I am definitely not a morning person. I don't know, I just don't think there's any situation where nobody could get hurt if I'm holding an open razor. I'm there, so I could get hurt. We ended up at this buffet at like 3 AM eating prime rib and crab legs. The crab legs were okay, but I've had way better in Maryland. My aunt lives in Annapolis, and she makes this crab dip that is just unbelievable. She puts Old Bay seasoning in everything. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Precision and control? Like, using it for something else... I don't think so. I mean, I know it's just a tool I trained with, right? I went to barbering school for a long time. The practical exams were brutal. We had this one instructor, Mr. Henderson, who would literally measure the fade lines with a little ruler to make sure they were perfectly symmetrical. He was so strict. He used to wear these immaculate three-piece suits every single day, even in the dead of summer when the AC in the building was broken. He said a barber must always present as a gentleman. He actually drove an old vintage Cadillac, one of those huge boats from the 70s, powder blue with white leather interior. It took up two parking spaces. He kept it in pristine condition, though. I think he spent more time waxing that car than he did at home. His wife actually used to bring him lunch sometimes, these little Tupperware containers full of the best-smelling Jamaican food. She made this jerk chicken that would make the whole breakroom smell amazing. I tried to get the recipe from her once, but she just laughed and said it was a family secret. I love cooking, though. I just bought an air fryer and I've been trying to make everything in it. I even tried making a cake in it last week, but it came out completely raw in the middle and burnt on the outside. It was a disaster...

Wait, sorry. The razor. Yeah, I can use clippers all day long without a second thought, but when that straight razor is sitting open at my station, it just feels like I'm about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. It doesn't matter what I'm using it for, the precision part doesn't help. The fear is just there.
